Theorem 221.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: For any algebraic_topology module M satisfying the convergence analysis hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 529. The projective resolution has length at most 723 with Betti numbers bounded by 1.

Proof: We establish this via a spectral argument. Note that by the convergence analysis decomposition theorem, the relevant homology group H_529(S, ≠) vanishes for dimension 529. The operator on the ambient space provides the required extension, and the obstruction class in H^723(S, F) is trivial. QED.

Theorem 221.1000 (Existence and Uniqueness)

Statement: Under the existence and uniqueness assumption on algebraic_topology structure S, the characteristic class c_k(S) vanishes for k > 179. The remaining classes generate a ring of rank 390 with relations in degree 2.

Proof: We establish this via a spectral argument. Note that by the existence and uniqueness decomposition theorem, the relevant homology group H_179(S, ≠) vanishes for dimension 179. The operator on the ambient space provides the required extension, and the obstruction class in H^390(S, F) is trivial. QED.

Theorem 221.1001 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: Every convergence analysis deformation of the algebraic_topology object S is unobstructed when dim H^1(S, T_S) < 193. The moduli space has dimension at most 16 with 1 connected components.

Proof: By the convergence analysis decomposition theorem, the relevant homology group H_193(S, ≠) vanishes for dimension 193. The extension on the ambient space provides the required map, and the obstruction class in H^16(S, F) is trivial. QED.
